## Idea

Bezout's lemma shows that for any two natural numbers `x` and `y` there exist
`k` and `l` such that `dist-ℕ (kx,ly) = gcd(x,y)`. To prove this, note that the
predicate `P : ℕ → UU lzero` given by

```text
  P z := Σ (k : ℕ), Σ (l : ℕ), dist-ℕ (kx, ly) = z
```

is decidable, because `P z ⇔ [y]_x | [z]_x` in `ℤ/x`. The least positive `z`
such that `P z` holds is `gcd x y`.

Bézout's Lemma is the [60th](literature.100-theorems.md#60) theorem on
[Freek Wiedijk](http://www.cs.ru.nl/F.Wiedijk/)'s list of
[100 theorems](literature.100-theorems.md) {{#cite 100theorems}}. It was
originally added to agda-unimath by [Bryan Lu](https://blu-bird.github.io).

## Lemmas

### If `z = dist-ℕ (kx, ly)` for some `k` and `l`, then `[y] | [z]` in `ℤ-Mod x`

If `z = dist-ℕ (kx, ly)` for some `k` and `l`, then it follows that
`ly ≡ ±z mod x`. It follows that `±ly ≡ z mod x`, and therefore that `[y] | [z]`
in `ℤ-Mod x`

### If `[y] | [z]` in `ℤ-Mod x`, then `z = dist-ℕ (kx, ly)` for some `k` and `l`

If `x = 0`, then we can simply argue in `ℤ`. Otherwise, if `[y] | [z]` in
`ℤ-Mod x`, there exists some equivalence class `u` in `ℤ-Mod x` such that
`[u] [y] ≡ [z]` as a congruence mod `x`. This `u` can always be chosen such that
`x > u ≥ 0`. Therefore, there exists some integer `a ≥ 0` such that
`ax = uy - z`, or `ax = z - uy`. In the first case, we can extract the distance
condition we desire. In the other case, we have that `ax + uy = z`. This can be
written as `(a + y)x + (u - x)y = z`, so that the second term is nonpositive.
Then, in this case, we again can extract the distance condition we desire.

## Theorem

Since `is-distance-between-multiples-ℕ x y z` is decidable, we can prove
Bezout's lemma by the well-ordering principle. First, take the least positive
distance `d` between multiples of `x` and `y`.

<pre class="Agda"><a id="pos-distance-between-multiples"></a><a id="29357" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29357" class="Function">pos-distance-between-multiples</a> <a id="29388" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="29390" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="29391" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29391" class="Bound">x</a> <a id="29393" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29393" class="Bound">y</a> <a id="29395" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29395" class="Bound">z</a> <a id="29397" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="29399" href="elementary-number-theory.natural-numbers.html#825" class="Datatype">ℕ</a><a id="29400" class="Symbol">)</a> <a id="29402" class="Symbol">→</a> <a id="29404" href="Agda.Primitive.html#388" class="Primitive">UU</a> <a id="29407" href="Agda.Primitive.html#915" class="Primitive">lzero</a>
<a id="29413" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29357" class="Function">pos-distance-between-multiples</a> <a id="29444" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29444" class="Bound">x</a> <a id="29446" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29446" class="Bound">y</a> <a id="29448" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29448" class="Bound">z</a> <a id="29450" class="Symbol">=</a> <a id="29452" href="elementary-number-theory.natural-numbers.html#1482" class="Function">is-nonzero-ℕ</a> <a id="29465" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="29466" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29444" class="Bound">x</a> <a id="29468" href="elementary-number-theory.addition-natural-numbers.html#907" class="Primitive Operator">+ℕ</a> <a id="29471" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29446" class="Bound">y</a><a id="29472" class="Symbol">)</a> <a id="29474" class="Symbol">→</a>
  <a id="29478" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="29479" href="elementary-number-theory.natural-numbers.html#1482" class="Function">is-nonzero-ℕ</a> <a id="29492" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29448" class="Bound">z</a><a id="29493" class="Symbol">)</a> <a id="29495" href="foundation-core.cartesian-product-types.html#585" class="Function Operator">×</a> <a id="29497" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="29498" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#2885" class="Function">is-distance-between-multiples-ℕ</a> <a id="29530" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29444" class="Bound">x</a> <a id="29532" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29446" class="Bound">y</a> <a id="29534" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29448" class="Bound">z</a><a id="29535" class="Symbol">)</a>

<a id="is-inhabited-pos-distance-between-multiples"></a><a id="29538" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29538" class="Function">is-inhabited-pos-distance-between-multiples</a> <a id="29582" class="Symbol">:</a>
  <a id="29586" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="29587" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29587" class="Bound">x</a> <a id="29589" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29589" class="Bound">y</a> <a id="29591" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="29593" href="elementary-number-theory.natural-numbers.html#825" class="Datatype">ℕ</a><a id="29594" class="Symbol">)</a> <a id="29596" class="Symbol">→</a> <a id="29598" href="foundation.dependent-pair-types.html#583" class="Record">Σ</a> <a id="29600" href="elementary-number-theory.natural-numbers.html#825" class="Datatype">ℕ</a> <a id="29602" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="29603" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29357" class="Function">pos-distance-between-multiples</a> <a id="29634" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29587" class="Bound">x</a> <a id="29636" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#29589" class="Bound">y</a><a id="29637" class="Symbol">)</a>

<a id="minimal-positive-distance"></a><a id="30596" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#30596" class="Function">minimal-positive-distance</a> <a id="30622" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="30624" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="30625" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#30625" class="Bound">x</a> <a id="30627" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#30627" class="Bound">y</a> <a id="30629" class="Symbol">:</a> <a id="30631" href="elementary-number-theory.natural-numbers.html#825" class="Datatype">ℕ</a><a id="30632" class="Symbol">)</a> <a id="30634" class="Symbol">→</a> <a id="30636" href="elementary-number-theory.natural-numbers.html#825" class="Datatype">ℕ</a>
<a id="30638" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#30596" class="Function">minimal-positive-distance</a> <a id="30664" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#30664" class="Bound">x</a> <a id="30666" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#30666" class="Bound">y</a> <a id="30668" class="Symbol">=</a> <a id="30670" href="foundation.dependent-pair-types.html#681" class="Field">pr1</a> <a id="30674" class="Symbol">(</a><a id="30675" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#30097" class="Function">minimal-pos-distance-between-multiples</a> <a id="30714" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#30664" class="Bound">x</a> <a id="30716" href="elementary-number-theory.bezouts-lemma-natural-numbers.html#30666" class="Bound">y</a><a id="30717" class="Symbol">)</a>
</pre>
Then `d` divides both `x` and `y`. Since `gcd x y` divides any number of the
form `dist-ℕ (kx,ly)`, it follows that `gcd x y | d`, and hence that
`gcd x y ＝ d`.
